# Values that exist on chain, and may not appear in a synthetic artifact.
#
# The rule (owner, 28 Sep 2026): **no value from a live deployment appears in any synthetic artifact,
# fixture, or page constant, ever.** It was written after E-14's demo fixture was found carrying the
# announced log's real transaction signature and slot beside a root built for the demo and published
# nowhere — and carrying them from two different publications at that. Fabricated provenance is the
# worst class of defect this repository can hold, and it is the kind an author cannot see in their own
# work.
#
# `cargo xtask gen-vectors` and `cargo xtask gen-demo` refuse to emit a file containing any value
# below, unless the pair is recorded in LIVE-VALUES.exempt. A vector that is *about* the announced
# deployment is legitimate and is exempted by name; a fixture that merely looks more concrete for
# carrying one is the defect.
#
# One value per line, then two spaces, then what it is. Hex is lowercase. Matching is
# case-insensitive, so a false positive is possible and fails generation, which is the safe direction.
#
# **This list is checked against the chain, not remembered.** Its first version was assembled by hand
# from what I could recall of the session that produced it, and it omitted **three of the six**
# signatures the deployment held at that point: epoch 20723's publication, which a review found, and
# the deploy and `initialize` transactions, which nothing had noticed. An earlier version of this note
# said two of five; a review counted and it was three of six. A list of live values built from memory
# has the same defect as a fixture built from memory, and a miscount in the note about it is the same
# defect once more.
#
# `scripts/live-values-from-chain.py` asks the cluster what touched the announced deployment and names
# anything missing here. **Run it after every publish cycle**: each adds a signature, a checkpoint
# address and a root.
#
# **What this gate cannot enforce, ruled by the owner 3 Oct 2026.** The rule above is unqualified and
# stays so, but two classes of live value cannot be held by it and are **review-enforced rather than
# gate-enforced**:
#
#   * **Bumps.** A PDA bump is 0-255. The announced deployment holds 250, 253 and 255, and 255 is also
#     the natural bump for any synthetic account because canonical search starts at 255 and counts down.
#   * **Epoch day indices.** Five digits, and a synthetic artifact may legitimately be *about* a real
#     epoch — the demo is about 20723.
#
# Listing either would make the matcher fire on unrelated text, because it matches a value as a
# case-insensitive substring of a whole file, and the exemptions that silenced the false positives are
# recorded per file **and value** rather than per occurrence — so one exemption blankets every
# occurrence in that file. That is why `check_list_shape` requires six digits or more.
#
# **The rule for these two classes, as the owner settled it on 4 Oct 2026 (PR #57, round four, M-03):**
#
#   A short live value may not appear in a synthetic artifact **unless the artifact's subject is the
#   announced deployment**, and each such use is recorded in this file, below, by file, value and
#   occurrence. A reviewer checks that record, because no gate can. (`LIVE-VALUES.exempt` is the wrong
#   home and refuses these entries: it exempts a *listed* value from the matcher, and these are values
#   the list cannot hold. The reasoning is with the record itself.)
#
# The earlier wording said only "a synthetic artifact uses a value no live account holds", and that was
# already false when it was written: the demo and its three fixtures are about epoch 20723, and V-P-12
# carries the live bumps 250 and 255 because §2.4's derivation against the announced deployment is the
# vector's whole subject. A reviewer had no predicate that admitted those and rejected the thing the
# rule is for, and could only apply it by importing the long-value exemption rule by eye.
#
# The distinction is the same one the long-value rule draws, and it is the only one that matters: an
# artifact whose subject is the deployment may name it; an artifact that merely looks more concrete for
# carrying one may not. A review found epoch 20723's real bump, 255, in `demo/test/footprint.test.mjs`
# while both halves of the gate exited 0 (PR #57, round two, H-01) — that fixture is not about the
# deployment, so it now uses 7, and the entries in `LIVE-VALUES.exempt` are the ones that are.
#
# **The short live values in use, recorded by file, value and occurrence.** This list is a reviewer's
# checklist, not gate input: these values are not listed above and cannot be, so nothing matches them.
# A short live value in a synthetic artifact that is *not* recorded here is one a reviewer should
# question. They are recorded here rather than in `LIVE-VALUES.exempt` because that file exempts a
# listed value from the matcher, and refuses an entry for a value the list does not hold — which these
# are, by construction. The gate rejected the first attempt to put them there, which is the right
# answer: an exemption from a check that never fires is not an exemption.
#
# The occurrence column is the raw number of times the value appears in that file, comments included,
# because `cargo xtask check-live-values` compares it against the tree and a qualified number cannot be
# checked. It caught this list's own first error: this entry said 4, counting uses and not the comment.
#
#   file                           value  occurrences  why
#   demo/app.js                    20723  1            Scene 1 rebuilds the announced log's first
#                                                      epoch, which is the scene's subject
#   demo/fixtures/root.json        20723  1            the committed root fixture for that scene
#   demo/fixtures/disclosure.json  20723  1            the package the scene verifies, same epoch
#   demo/fixtures/tampered.json    20723  1            the altered copy of that package
#   demo/test/footprint.test.mjs   20723  5            the scene's own account and two instructions,
#                                                      plus the comment explaining why the slot that
#                                                      used to sit beside them is gone
#   vectors/V-P-12.json            20723  2            §2.4's derivation is checked against this
#                                                      deployment's epoch-20723 checkpoint, which is
#                                                      the vector's whole subject
#   vectors/V-P-12.json            250    1            the LogConfig bump the deployment holds, which
#                                                      the derivation must reproduce
#   vectors/V-P-12.json            255    3            the checkpoint bump, one-seed and two-seed —
#                                                      and a third match inside `Ed25519`, which is the
#                                                      carve-out in one line, below
#
# **A third "255" in V-P-12.json is inside `Ed25519`, and that is the carve-out in one line.** A
# matcher looking for the bump 255 as a substring finds the curve's name, a slot index, a record count
# and a byte value, and a rule that fires on all of those gets switched off. That is why these are
# read rather than matched, and what H-20 has to solve before they can be listed.
#
# This is a stated boundary on enforcement, not on the rule. H-20
# ([#60](https://github.com/CertiMining/potash/issues/60)) makes the matcher token-aware so these values
# can be listed safely, and when it lands this carve-out is withdrawn. H-27 and H-28 are the two
# consequences that depend on it.

# Values stored *inside* those accounts (review of PR #121, H-01). Listing the 215 addresses
# did not list what they hold. A review decoded all 215 under the layouts this repository
# uses — 213 checkpoints and 2 LogConfigs, nothing unrecognised — and found 640 values no
# check had ever asked for: 213 roots, 213 publication slots, 213 publication timestamps and
# one non-zero receipt digest. Inserting one of those roots into a fuzz target left
# `check-live-values` exiting 0 while it printed that the surface carried no value this list
# holds. Enumerating addresses is not enumerating values, and the claim was the louder defect.
#
# Two of the 640 travelled the other way. The 24 September deployment's single checkpoint
# holds `0xabab…abab` and `0x3333…3333`, which are this repository's own placeholder constants:
# `crates/certimining-client/tests/devnet.rs` published them to a throwaway program. They are
# listed anyway, because the rule is unqualified and narrowing it is not a tool's decision, and
# the files that legitimately carry them are recorded in `LIVE-VALUES.exempt` by exact path.
